# November 5 — C054 text-color contrast review

**1 new source/browser-tested feature;0 separate fixes/scaffolding additions.** Drawing → Objects → Review text colors compares all visible text’s assigned color against an explicitly chosen opaque hexadecimal background. Source color alpha and object opacity are composited over that background before sRGB luminance calculation. Hidden/deleted text is excluded;locked text remains included. Stable text IDs/numbers lead to the existing nonmutating Find on canvas action. Object-list filters do not narrow this all-text review. Results/settings stay transient and do not edit projects/media or create Undo entries.

Uses the W3C [G18 formula](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Techniques/general/G18) and [unrounded threshold guidance](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/contrast-minimum.html),verified November5.4.5:1 is a normal-text color reference only;no final font/size or WCAG conformance claim. Near-threshold display cannot round a failing score up into a passing label. This tool does not sample actual pixels or infer backgrounds behind text,photos,shapes,patterns,groups or effects. UI keeps a short limitation visible and places the detailed explanation in a keyboard-operable disclosure. Users still review the finished output.

## Native timeline / layered photo checkpoint — November 4

Nine further source workflows after the live-editor eleven: C019 sort whole timeline clips by source time or playback duration (one workflow, not three); C020 remove exactly duplicate clips while retaining different owned edits; C021 join source-contiguous cuts only when render/audio/title/layout settings agree and edge fades are absent; C022 extend selected caption holds into gaps without overlap, in source milliseconds; C023 caption letter-case transformation preserving timing/provenance and refusing Unicode expansion beyond limits; C024 read-only timeline settings report, expressly not audio/visual content analysis; C005 independently editable native photo shape objects; C006 multiple independently editable native text objects; C025 save/restore/delete editable photo state for an existing accessible original. Native photo objects: a defensive immutable collection capped at 32 objects; bounded normalized geometry, rotation, opacity, visibility and locking; output RGB color; text capped at 200 characters / three lines. Shared PhotoRasterRenderer draws objects after composition/caption rendering. Source pixels remain separate. Source-relative geometry plans are used consistently for preview and export. Native controls support object add/edit/duplicate/order/hide/lock/delete, with stale-editor/account guards. Lock/order/hide/duplicate controls are supporting object behavior and are not separate new feature counts. Rich histories are pruned to the existing 64 KiB lifecycle budget while keeping current and pending export state. Saved adjustment looks retain the target photo's objects.

Photo projects: private `noBackupFilesDir/photo-projects/<validated namespace>/` files; up to 20 originals per guest/account namespace, hashed filenames, no stored raw source URI or account token/media bytes. A source SHA-256 (bounded 128 MiB read) and envelope digest protect restoration against changed originals/corruption. Requires durable document access or preexisting broad MediaStore photo access; no new broad permission prompt. Newly acquired document access rolls back on failure, prior grants remain. Atomic writes are verified; corrupt or changed-source saves require explicit delete before replacement. Worker/scope guards keep current edits intact on failures/account changes. Restore applies one undoable edit. Cache-generated source images are explicitly excluded until exported and reopened through Files; C008 durable canvas recovery remains pending. This is local editable state, not a claim of encrypted cloud/account drafts or source backup.

## C008 native durable canvas project checkpoint — November 4

Implemented the formerly missing native created-image recovery path. CanvasProjectCodec is a bounded ID/source-digest/name/edit-state envelope reusing PhotoSessionCodec. CanvasProjectStore copies a Noble-created PNG source losslessly into private `files/canvas-projects/<UUID>/source.png`, with metadata in that same project directory. It validates PNG type, maximum 4096 per side / 4,194,304 pixels, source read budget, metadata identity/digest, unique names and a maximum of 20 projects. New projects commit a staging directory only after source sync and metadata verification; failed creation removes only its own staging. ## C026/C027 typography source checkpoint — November 4

Two new native photo workflows: C026 circular-path editable text, and C027 text appearance (platform font family, bold/italic, alignment, tracking, outline and shadow as one styling workflow). # November 4 — C043 private local video voiceovers (experimental source)

**1 new source workflow:** Video → Record voiceover opens an explicit foreground microphone recorder, reviews a take, privately keeps it, and optionally uses it as the canonical project soundtrack. Permission is requested only on Start; granting permission never starts capture. Leaving cancels unfinished recording/review. Saved takes have Preview/Use/confirmed Delete; deleting warns about saved-project dependencies. Capture is limited to three minutes/8MiB with20 saved takes per owner. No audio was captured during validation.

Actual Android MediaRecorder AAC source writes only owned pending files, verifies audio metadata before keeping, and promotes to UUID originals under owner-scoped no-backup storage. A private, non-exported, no-grant, read-only ContentProvider exposes only validated saved IDs to the app. Active account checks, permission/foreground guards, a single recording lease, known-file cleanup and canonical-path checks protect the workflow. Guest durable-project access recognizes only canonical owned readable voiceover URIs. No microphone hardware is required to install; saved takes remain usable without one. No network/upload, credit or native search change.

Voiceover attachment checks the unchanged project/account snapshot and confirms replacement of existing music. Narration plays once from project start at the existing25% soundtrack default; original clip audio stays attached and Undo restores prior audio. Draftv8 stores one-shot audio while older music defaults to looping and keeps prior codec versions. Shared preview/export source clips narration to project length, and recovery-budget checks run before history mutations.

XML privacy/optional-microphone declarations pass;9 changed Kotlin files pass syntax parsing only. C068 is one distinct color-mapping result, not one feature per endpoint/control. PhotoDuotone validates two opaque 24-bit RGB endpoints and finite0..1 blend;pure parseHex accepts trimmed #RRGGBB only. The scalar kernel derives Rec.709-weighted encoded-RGB luminance and interpolates encoded RGB endpoints, then blends with the input. It preserves source alpha exactly;disabled/zero-amount/invisible pixels are exact identities. It is not a linear-light/HDR transform. The shared row renderer runs after existing monochrome and before person background/transparency fade/composition/regions/caption/objects. Added elements thus retain their own colors;later local regions may recolor mapped pixels. No original file/URI/provenance mutation added.

Native white/black controls offer enable, shadow/highlight HEX,Swap,percent blend and API30stateDescription. Inputs validate before commit;Cancel does not call the model. Captured loaded scope/edit and existing photoControlUnchanged guard reject stale commits;unchanged settings skip history. Actual controls/HEX keyboard/error/cancel/accessibility/Undo gesture paths are source-reviewed, not Android-executed.

An actualv13 binary fixture was produced with the pre-changeC066 API before edits,then decoded and reencoded exactly by the new codec. Existing actualv1/v12 tests also pass. # November 5 — C070 local suggested photo framing

**1 new experimental source workflow; 0 separate fixes or scaffolding.** Objects → Suggest crop uses pinned MIT Smartcrop on the full embedded photo copy. Choose square, portrait, wide or vertical framing, inspect the photo-only preview, then Apply or Keep current framing. The crop fits within the old photo box and keeps its center, rotation, flip and opacity. Source bytes stay unchanged; the existing crop field supports Undo, recovery and exports. The UI explicitly distinguishes full-source project/SVG files from flattened PNG artwork.

**118 model tests pass (5 new)**, plus the actual framing browser flow and existing photo-replacement regression. The browser checks Cancel, one-step Undo/Redo/no-op, source-byte preservation, reload/project JSON, crop-region and flip/rotation agreement in preview/PNG/SVG, three asynchronous source fences, phone widths and bundled license. No Android assets refresh, full native/Gradle/APK build, real-device or crop-quality acceptance is claimed. Source register: **69/500; batch 31/100** beyond 38; target~138. See [feature evidence](FEATURE_C070_PHOTO_FRAMING.md) and `.context/noble-fast-photo-framing/verification.json`. Main's sharing and playback lifecycle fixes and their runtime limitations remain intact.

C070 adds a content-aware suggestion workflow, not another manual crop preset or a platform copy. The audit found existing manual image crop fields and center-crop tools but no automatic framing suggestion. The four ratios are parameters of this one workflow.

Runtime Smartcrop 2.0.5 is pinned with its MIT notice in creation-web/THIRD_PARTY/SMARTCROP-LICENSE.txt and the existing offline bundle. Only the selected embedded PNG/JPEG copy is decoded; natural dimensions must match validated bytes, with at least 32 px on each side and the existing 1024 px/embedded-byte bounds. The algorithm analyzes the full source using minScale=maxScale=1. It is a visual heuristic, not face recognition or a guarantee of good composition. No source URLs, queries or pixels are uploaded.

The parent captures the editor identity, complete encoded scene and file data. Each decode/analysis boundary and Apply checks freshness. The preview component fences unmounts and obsolete requests. Apply changes the existing crop and fitted geometry only; no new file or schema is created. Crop bounds are finite and positive, with only tiny floating-point edge overshoot normalized to the source boundary. Protected, hidden, grouped, bound or changed photos are rejected through the existing photo admission helper. Reapplying the same crop skips an empty Undo step. The preview renders crop/flip/rotation/opacity rather than silently showing the untransformed image.

Five model families cover fitting/center/appearance/source preservation, no-op and previous-crop replacement, invalid geometry/dimensions, protected/stale targets and supported ratio geometry. All 118 drawing-model families pass. Actual browser tests use synthetic 800×600 photo data only: the suggestion selects x 168,y 0,width 600,height 600; no scene change occurs before Apply; Cancel preserves the scene; source file bytes and the protected photo remain exact; Undo/Redo and no-op reapplication work; Save/reload and project JSON retain the crop. Actual preview/PNG/SVG content ratios and normalized colored-region centroids agree, checking both selected source region and flip/rotation. Three real service fences reject before decode, after decode and after analysis. The existing replacement browser regression also passes.

SVG export intentionally embeds the full photo behind its crop; the test confirms it retains the source data URI. The UI discloses this before Apply. Use flattened PNG artwork when only the visible rendering is wanted; this is not a secure-redaction claim. Real Android WebView/SAF/lifecycle, printing, visual quality, performance and device acceptance remain pending. No native/full-app/Gradle/APK build, account/ledger/ranking/search change or benchmark run. A temporary browser cache was lost after restart and restored in this task's own /tmp directory. The first recovery assertion reloaded an unsaved Redo; the harness was corrected to Save explicitly, with no production change for that failure. Source hashes, test logs, license, synthetic exports and screenshots are retained under .context/noble-fast-photo-framing/.


# November 5 — C071 transparent-border trim

**1 new experimental source workflow; 0 separate fixes or scaffolding.** Photo editor → Transform → Trim transparency creates an independent flattened PNG from the edited render after removing only fully transparent outer rows and columns. Faint edges stay; opaque or empty images receive an explanation. Current editable work, Undo history and original-source protection stay in the original workspace. The confirmation explains preview resolution and saving/exporting the temporary result. Foreground/session guards cancel stale work. **72 photo/shared source files pass API compilation and 287 host tests pass**, including nine new bounds/admission cases. Evidence: `.context/photo-alpha-trim/verification.json`; details: [FEATURE_C071_ALPHA_TRIM.md](FEATURE_C071_ALPHA_TRIM.md).

**70/500 source workflows; 32/100 added since baseline 38. Next full-build target remains approximately 138.** No Android bitmap/PNG/dialog/provider/navigation or device execution, no full React Native/Gradle/native/APK build, and no bundled drawing-asset refresh. Bounds and paused admission are host-tested; actual image output and original integrity need later device acceptance. C070 and all prior work are preserved. Group-chat progress and coordination remain active.

The source inventory had manual centre cropping and directional transparency fades, but no operation to remove empty transparent borders while preserving all visible pixels. C071 is one distinct result, not a crop preset or a parameter count. The row scan bounds allocations to 4,194,304 pixels and reuses one IntArray. Only alpha zero is excluded; even alpha 1 contributes to the bounds. The Android adapter uses getPixels by row and Bitmap.createBitmap for the detected rectangle. It refuses empty and already-tight results. Current edited output is rendered first, so the new source is intentionally flattened rather than a new edit-schema field.

Publication reuses the bounded private-image writer and protected-original provenance used by the comparison workflow. It carries no account privileges into the guest workspace. Current photo, edit, source, owner scope and foreground generation must still match before publication and launch. Backgrounding cancels the request even after foreground return. Worker rejection, failure, stale completion and failed launch retain current edits; private output cleanup follows existing writer rules. The new transform button occupies a separate row so it does not consume the existing controls' row width. Actual large-font/touch layout is still pending.

The fresh photo API and base tests were compiled against API34, AndroidX Core and existing verified ML dependencies. After the foreground guard, admission case and wording/layout refinements, the final Activity and trim-test classes were recompiled and placed before the unchanged base jars. All 287 tests pass on that final classpath; source and dependency digests are checked. Eight new cases test the pixel-bound model, including fixed-seed synthetic masks; one constructor-free actual-Activity case proves paused admission returns before UI access. No bitmap pixels or dialog execution occurs in those host tests. Earlier successful and failed harness logs remain available. A missing output-directory shell attempt was corrected before validation; it did not change app behavior.

The result is a separate local photo workspace: existing save/project/PNG export can retain it, while returning to the previous workspace keeps its original editable state. This is source integration, not proof of Android navigation, actual PNG alpha, renderer/crop parity, cache lifecycle, source byte integrity or save/provider behavior. Those require synthetic-device acceptance at the owner-approved build checkpoint. Search ranking, holdout, billing/credits, private-media upload rules and all existing source edits are untouched. No new runtime dependency or edit schema. The group chat records the milestones and preserves C070's ownership and committed notes.

# November 5 — C073 outlined sticker creation

**1 new experimental source workflow; 0 separate fixes or scaffolding.** Photo editor → Transform → Make outlined sticker creates a padded, rounded white/black border around transparent edited artwork and opens a separate local PNG workspace. Border width is 2–32 preview pixels. The kernel retains original visible ARGB, including faint edges. The dialog explains flattened preview resolution, preserving the existing editable project and saving/exporting the temporary copy. TinySDF v2.0.7's BSD distance-transform routine is adapted to Kotlin, with full notices retained and Noble's white/black controls. **73 photo/shared source files pass API compilation; 299 host tests pass**, including 12 new sticker cases and existing C071 regressions. Actual kernel outputs also round-trip through host PNG and were visually inspected. Evidence: `.context/photo-sticker/verification.json`; details: [FEATURE_C073_STICKER.md](FEATURE_C073_STICKER.md).

**72/500 source workflows; 34/100 added since baseline 38. Next broad build target remains approximately 138.** No Android bitmap/PNG/dialog/provider/navigation or device execution, no whole-app/Gradle/native/APK build, and no drawing-assets refresh. Host PNG evidence is not Android output proof. Current edits and source protection are preserved in code; actual source integrity and project/export behavior still need device acceptance. C072 and all prior work are retained. The group chat remains active for claims, progress and handoffs.

The inventory had text outlines, edge filters, frames and alpha trimming, but no rounded sticker asset following a transparent artwork's alpha shape. C073 is one workflow; colors and radius values are not additional counts. Opaque/empty sources refuse before creating an asset. The source must fit 4,194,304 pixels; padded output is bounded to 8,388,608 pixels and 4096 per edge. The model trims only empty outer space, pads the result, computes squared distances to all nonzero-alpha pixels and paints only previously transparent pixels. It preserves foreground source values and discards hidden RGB in empty regions. Anti-aliased circular coverage follows Euclidean rather than square distance. The copied routine uses Float grid storage, Double scratch/intersections and bounded reusable arrays. Cancellation checks occur across scan, placement, distance and paint stages.

Open-source reuse: [TinySDF v2.0.7](https://github.com/mapbox/tiny-sdf/tree/v2.0.7), BSD-2-Clause. The exact upstream source hash and full license are recorded. The Kotlin header and shared Android asset retain the notice; React Native includes that shared asset root through its existing source set. An initially added duplicate ReactNative-root notice was removed after checking the source-set overlap. Both apps are configured to receive one notice; actual APK merging remains unexecuted. No JavaScript/font engine or new runtime graphics package is embedded. This follows the owner's instruction to reuse and restyle useful open-source components without importing unnecessary infrastructure.

C071 and C073 now share the guarded image-copy transaction. Selection settings are frozen at confirmation; current photo/edit/source chain/session/foreground generation must match through publication. Worker rejection, cancellation and failed launch retain current edits and clean private output. The new copy has no upstream layers or editable border parameters; the original workspace remains the place to adjust and regenerate. The native view uses the existing checkerboard for alpha-bearing source images so white sticker borders can be seen. Both transform tools use their own rows. Actual dialog, touch, large-font, TalkBack, bitmap/color/PNG, original hashes, cache/persistence and navigation are still pending.

Twelve new host cases cover single-pixel padding, exact visible ARGB/source preservation, hidden RGB, rounded geometry, color, invalid allocation and radius, opaque/empty inputs, output size, cancellation, 60 fixed-seed masks against an independent brute-force reference, and paused actual-Activity admission without Android UI. The complete source API and 299 host cases pass with source/dependency hashes unchanged. A separate host-only Kotlin gallery calls the actual kernel and encodes/decodes PNG with Java ImageIO; its ARGB arrays match and the gallery was inspected. This does not execute the Android adapter or codec. Temporary Android/Core/JUnit tools from the previous turn had disappeared; restored the declared public artifacts into a private task cache, retaining the failed setup log. Dependency hashes were stable before checks completed. No app dependencies, edit schemas, search/ranking, holdout, credits or personal-media upload behavior changed. No full build or deployment.

# November 5 — Before/after comparison foreground guard

**0 new features; 1 correctness fix; 0 scaffolding.** The older comparison tool now refuses paused admission and captures the current foreground generation and original-source chain. Existing dialog/worker/publication checks reject a stale request, including leaving and returning to the same editor. Both layouts, current edits and provenance remain unchanged. A constructor-free Activity regression failed on the old paused path and passes after the guard. **301 photo host tests pass**, including two new admission cases, and the current Activity API override compiles against the verified photo API. Evidence: `.context/photo-comparison-admission/verification.json`; details: [CREATIVE_500_WORKLOG.md](CREATIVE_500_WORKLOG.md).

**73/500 source workflows; 35/100 added since baseline 38. Target remains approximately 138 before the next broad build.** No actual Android dialogs, foreground callbacks, comparison output, providers or device execution; no whole-app/Gradle/native/APK build or drawing-assets refresh. The group-chat browser server was restored in an independent process session and its live board API was verified. All prior feature notes and other-chat work are preserved.

The C065 path predated C071/C073's foreground fence. Its captured current() predicate checked photo, edit, source and account scope, but not pause/reentry generation. The new guard uses the same copyForeground/copyGeneration state already maintained by the Activity. It also requires the captured inherited-original list to remain unchanged. Therefore a delayed comparison cannot publish a child workspace after a pause or provenance change. Existing file cleanup and busy completion paths remain in place; computation is not guaranteed to stop immediately during an Android render or encode.

Two new tests exercise actual Activity admission through the existing constructor-free host fixture. The old paused fixture was otherwise eligible to finish, reached scope/UI handling and failed on its deliberately absent note view. That demonstrates missing early admission, not an observed Android crash or actual dialog. The new paused guard returns first; foreground busy/closed variants still return before UI. The current Activity and new test are compiled into an override jar before the verified C073 API and base test jars. All 301 current photo host cases pass, including trim/sticker regressions. Cached unchanged source/dependency hashes are checked; no source/model ABI, dependency, schema, search/ranking, credit or original-media behavior is intentionally changed. Foreground callback delivery, asynchronous cancellation, file bytes and real navigation remain later device acceptance.

The group-chat API was found unavailable during this milestone. Restored the server with detached Node spawn and a separate process session rather than a shell background job, then verified its board API from separate calls. Claims, progress and results were posted in plain language. This is an operational restoration, not a new app feature. No other agent's claim was released or file overwritten. Full logs and before/after evidence are retained.

# November 5 — Bounded native photo preview

**0 new features; 1 reliability fix; 0 scaffolding.** The native photo preview now reuses PhotoSourceDecoder with a 2024-pixel limit and its existing memory/pixel budget on a worker thread. It shows loading and recoverable error messages. The displayed bitmap has explicit ownership; closing detaches the drawable before release, and late results are discarded. Existing video playback state is preserved. **16 focused host tests pass**: four new ownership cases, seven existing video-state cases and five existing decode-budget cases. The current Activity, decoder and models also pass an isolated API compile without an insets stub. Evidence: `.context/media-photo-preview/verification.json`; details: [CREATIVE_500_WORKLOG.md](CREATIVE_500_WORKLOG.md).

**74/500 source workflows; 36/100 added since baseline 38. Next broad build target remains approximately 138.** Actual Android decoding, EXIF orientation, display, bitmap release, rotation/close behavior and memory pressure remain untested on a device. No full app/Gradle/native/APK build or drawing-assets refresh. Source files and existing tests are preserved; the group chat has the milestone update.

The old image branch called ImageView.setImageURI directly during Activity creation, without an explicit preview decode limit or worker error feedback. It now calls the same bounded, read-only decoder used by photo editing. The decoder probes dimensions, chooses a sample size from the requested long-edge and available-memory/pixel budgets, and applies its existing EXIF orientation handling. This preview never exports or rewrites the original. A late or failed result cannot substitute another library item.

MediaPreviewImage owns the accepted decoded bitmap, releases a replaced resource, ignores duplicate publication of the same resource, and closes idempotently. Actual ImageView use currently publishes one decoded result; destruction detaches that drawable before disposing it. The executor stops accepting work on destruction, and a completed decode for a closed/finishing/replaced view is recycled instead of displayed. Native video position/pause behavior remains in the existing playback model. No new dependency, schema, search/ranking or billing change is introduced.

The host checks execute ownership with dummy resources, video-state logic and decode-budget mathematics. They do not execute BitmapFactory, EXIF streams, ImageView, actual bitmap recycling or Android lifecycle callbacks. The exact Activity and decoder source compile against Android API34 and the verified photo API, with the real cached insets ABI rather than a stub. The saved process handle was no longer available on resumption, but completed logs, checks and matching current source hashes established the result; no redundant run was started. Whole-app integration, actual large images/rotation/close races, rendering and original-byte integrity still need synthetic-device acceptance at the owner-approved build checkpoint. Full logs and source hashes are retained.

# November 5 — Accessible media viewer controls

**0 new features; 1 accessibility fix; 0 scaffolding.** Viewer actions now sit beside the full-screen media tap target instead of inside it. The surface has labelled Show/Hide viewer controls actions, Close has an explicit button role, and accessibility escape uses the existing close callback. Open/Edit/Share have 48-unit minimum touch areas, wrapping and disabled feedback. **146 React Native tests pass**, including three new semantic checks; typecheck passes and lint reports 0 errors/41 warnings. The two original grouping/toggle regressions fail before the change and pass afterward. Evidence: `.context/viewer-accessibility/verification.json`; details: [CREATIVE_500_WORKLOG.md](CREATIVE_500_WORKLOG.md).

**75/500 source workflows; 37/100 added since baseline 38. Next broad build target remains approximately 138.** No native accessibility-tree/layout, TalkBack/VoiceOver, actual escape gestures, focus order, large-font or touch/device execution. No whole-app/Gradle/native/APK build or drawing-assets refresh. Source hierarchy and callbacks are host-tested; actual accessibility acceptance remains pending. All prior work and other-chat milestones are preserved.

The installed React Native Pressable implementation defaults accessible to true. The old media surface was an interactive ancestor of Close/Open/Edit/Share, which can group descendants and makes screen-reader navigation ambiguous. The new noninteractive canvas contains the separately labelled media surface and overlay controls as siblings. Hiding controls leaves the media surface accessible with a Show action. Escape and Modal onRequestClose both call the existing close callback; image cropping, action payloads, pending guards and reduced-motion choice are unchanged.

The new tests reject an interactive ancestor around controls, toggle labelled Show/Hide actions and verify close/escape callbacks. Existing viewer, picker, account/search and entitlement tests remain in the 146-pass suite. They do not emulate actual screen-reader focus or physical layout. Source action minima and wrapping are configured, not device-measured. The old semantic checks failed as expected. An initial cold test hit its existing 5-second limit; the public Modal type is now resolved during fixture setup and reused in the exit-path assertion. The final full suite used a fresh Jest cache and passed without relaxing per-test timeouts. A typecheck caught absoluteFillObject being absent from the pinned SDK; replaced it with explicit absolute bounds and reran all checks. Failed logs remain retained. A patch-script syntax error occurred before any source write and was corrected before final validation.

Restored the exact existing mobile dependency lock to its missing temporary target using npm ci, with cache on the temporary disk. No app package/lock changes, new runtime dependency, source media access/upload, search ranking or credit changes. Shared group-chat messages record progress and scope. Actual Android/iOS accessibility, large-font layout, gesture delivery and full app integration remain later device acceptance under the existing build cadence.
